Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is defined as either:
   - A constant m-bit number, or
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's number
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to get minimum possible score
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to get maximum possible score

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently due to the properties of bitwise operations and linearity of sums.

2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can consider the contribution of that bit to the total sum independently.

3. In each bit position, we have 0 or 1 values, and we can determine what value of Peter's bit (0 or 1) minimizes or maximizes the total sum for that position.

4. Since we want the minimum number when there are ties, we should choose 0 if both options give the same sum.

## Algorithm

1. For each bit position (from most significant to least significant):
   - Simulate the value of each variable at that bit position
   - The value depends on whether Peter's bit is 0 or 1
   - Calculate the total contribution to the sum for each choice of Peter's bit
   - Choose the bit that minimizes/maximizes the sum (prefer 0 in case of ties)

2. To evaluate the effect of Peter's bit at position i:
   - For each variable, determine its value at position i given Peter's bit is 0 or 1
   - Sum these values across all variables
   - Compare the sums for bit 0 vs bit 1

3. For each bit position, we can simulate the computation by treating each bit as a separate binary field.

## Step-by-step Solution

After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Variables can be defined as:
   - Constant m-bit binary numbers
   - Result of bitwise operations (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's chosen number `?`
3. Peter chooses an m-bit number that minimizes or maximizes the sum of all variable values
4. If multiple choices give the same sum, we want the minimum number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently due to the properties of bitwise operations.

2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine what value of Peter's bit (0 or 1) will minimize/maximize the contribution to the total sum.

3. The XOR operation is particularly important because it's not linear in the same way as AND/OR.

4. For a given bit position, if we fix Peter's bit to 0 or 1, we can compute the contribution of that bit to the total sum.

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input to get variable definitions
2. For each bit position from 0 to m-1:
   - Simulate what happens when Peter's bit is 0
   - Simulate what happens when Peter's bit is 1
   - Calculate the total contribution of that bit position to the sum
3. Choose the bit value (0 or 1) that minimizes/maximizes the sum for each position
4. Combine the results

## Implementation Strategy

For each bit position, I'll:
1. Create a function to evaluate the value of a variable given Peter's bit at that position
2. For each variable, compute its value at each bit position
3. Sum up the contributions from all variables at that bit position
4. Choose the bit (0 or 1) that gives the minimum/maximum contribution

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Parse variable definitions
    variables = {}
    definitions = []
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" =:=")
        name = parts[0].strip()
        expr = parts[1].strip()
        
        # Extract left and right parts
        right_part = expr.split()
        if len(right_part) == 1:
            # Constant
            value = right_part[0]
        else:
            # Operation expression
            op_index = 2
            first_operand = right_part[0]
            op = right_part[1]
            second_operand = right_part[2]
            
            value = (first_operand, op, second_operand)
        
        definitions.append((name, value))
    
    # For each bit position, calculate contribution when Peter's bit is 0 vs 1
    min_bits = []
    max_bits = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Calculate contribution of this bit to total sum when Peter's bit is 0
        contrib_0 = 0
        # Calculate contribution of this bit to total sum when Peter's bit is 1
        contrib_1 = 0
        
        # For each variable, compute its value at this bit position
        # We need to track the value of each variable at this bit position
        var_values = {}
        
        for name, expr in definitions:
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                first, op, second = expr
                # Get values of operand variables
                val1 = 0
                val2 = 0
                
                if first == '?':
                    val1 = 0  # When Peter's bit is 0
                else:
                    val1 = get_bit_value_at_position(first, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if second == '?':
                    val2 = 0  # When Peter's bit is 0
                else:
                    val2 = get_bit_value_at_position(second, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                # Apply operation
                if op == "AND":
                    result = val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                
                var_values[name] = result
            else:
                # Constant
                if len(expr) == m:
                    val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
                    var_values[name] = val
        
        # Add contribution to total sum when Peter's bit is 0
        for name, expr in definitions:
            val = 0
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                first, op, second = expr
                if first == '?':
                    val1 = 0
                else:
                    val1 = get_bit_value_at_position(first, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if second == '?':
                    val2 = 0
                else:
                    val2 = get_bit_value_at_position(second, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if op == "AND":
                    result = val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                val = result
            else:
                val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
            
            contrib_0 += val * (1 << bit_pos)
        
        # Now do the same for Peter's bit = 1
        var_values = {}
        
        for name, expr in definitions:
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                first, op, second = expr
                # Get values of operand variables
                val1 = 0
                val2 = 0
                
                if first == '?':
                    val1 = 1  # When Peter's bit is 1
                else:
                    val1 = get_bit_value_at_position(first, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if second == '?':
                    val2 = 1  # When Peter's bit is 1
                else:
                    val2 = get_bit_value_at_position(second, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                # Apply operation
                if op == "AND":
                    result = val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                
                var_values[name] = result
            else:
                # Constant
                if len(expr) == m:
                    val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
                    var_values[name] = val
        
        # Add contribution to total sum when Peter's bit is 1
        for name, expr in definitions:
            val = 0
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                first, op, second = expr
                if first == '?':
                    val1 = 1
                else:
                    val1 = get_bit_value_at_position(first, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if second == '?':
                    val2 = 1
                else:
                    val2 = get_bit_value_at_position(second, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if op == "AND":
                    result = val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                val = result
            else:
                val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
            
            contrib_1 += val * (1 << bit_pos)
        
        # Choose the bit that minimizes the sum
        if contrib_0 <= contrib_1:
            min_bits.append('0')
        else:
            min_bits.append('1')
        
        # Choose the bit that maximizes the sum
        if contrib_0 >= contrib_1:
            max_bits.append('0')
        else:
            max_bits.append('1')
    
    # Convert bit lists to binary strings
    min_result = ''.join(min_bits)
    max_result = ''.join(max_bits)
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)

# After more thought, here's the clean and correct solution:

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store the definitions in a list
    # Each element is (name, type, args)
    # type: "const" or "op"
    # for const: (name, "const", constant_string)
    # for op: (name, op, operand1, operand2)
    
    operations = []
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        # Split on ":="
        parts = line.split(" := ", 1)
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        # If it's a constant, it's a single string
        # If it's an operation, it's "A op B"
        
        # Split by spaces
        expr_parts = expr.split()
        
        if len(expr_parts) == 1:
            # Constant
            operations.append((var_name, "const", expr_parts[0]))
        else:
            # Operation
            if len(expr_parts) == 3:
                first, op, second = expr_parts[0], expr_parts[1], expr_parts[2]
                operations.append((var_name, op, first, second))
    
    # For each bit position
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    for bit in range(m):
        # Calculate the total sum value when Peter's bit is 0
        # and when Peter's bit is 1
        
        # For each variable, compute its value at this bit position
        def get_value(var_name, peter_bit):
            # Returns the bit value (0 or 1) of the variable at this position
            if var_name == "?" or var_name == "peter":
                return peter_bit
            
            # Find this variable in operations
            for var, op_type, *args in operations:
                if var == var_name:
                    if op_type == "const":
                        # This should be the constant string
                        # Extract the bit at 'bit' position
                        const_str = args[0]
                        return int(const_str[bit])
                    else:  # op_type is an operation
                        first, op, second = args
                        a_val = get_value(first, peter_bit)
                        b_val = get_value(second, peter_bit)
                        
                        if op == "AND":
                            return a_val & b_val
                        elif op == "OR":
                            return a_val | b_val
                        elif op == "XOR":
                            return a_val ^ b_val
            return 0
        
        # When Peter's bit is 0
        total_0 = 0
        for var_name, op_type, *args in operations:
            val = get_value(var_name, 0)
            total_0 += val * (1 << bit)
        
        # When Peter's bit is 1
        total_1 = 0
        for var_name, op_type, *args in operations:
            val = get_value(var_name, 1)
            total_1 += val * (1 << bit)
        
        # Choose bit that minimizes the sum
        if total_0 <= total_1:
            min_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
        
        # Choose bit that maximizes the sum
        if total_0 >= total_1:
            max_result.append('0')
        else:
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Output results
    print(''.join(min_result))
    print(''.join(max_result))

solve()
```

                if len(expr) == m:
                    val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
                    var_values[name] = val
        
        # Add contribution to total sum when Peter's bit is 1
        for name, expr in definitions:
            val = 0
            if isinstance(expr, tuple):
                first, op, second = expr
                if first == '?':
                    val1 = 1
                else:
                    val1 = get_bit_value_at_position(first, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if second == '?':
                    val2 = 1
                else:
                    val2 = get_bit_value_at_position(second, bit_pos, var_values)
                
                if op == "AND":
                    result = val1 & val2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result = val1 | val2
                elif op == "XOR":
                    result = val1 ^ val2
                val = result
            else:
                val = int(expr[bit_pos], 2)
            
            contrib_1 += val * (1 << bit_pos)
        
        # Choose the bit that minimizes the sum
        if contrib_0 <= contrib_1:
            min_bits.append('0')
        else:
            min_bits.append('1')
        
        # Choose the bit that maximizes the sum
        if contrib_0 >= contrib_1:
            max_bits.append('0')
        else:
            max_bits.append('1')
    
    # Convert bit lists to binary strings
    min_result = ''.join(min_bits)
    max_result = ''.join(max_bits)
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)

def solve():

# After more thought, here's the clean and correct solution:

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store the definitions in a list
    # Each element is (name, type, args)
    # type: "const" or "op"
    # for const: (name, "const", constant_string)
    # for op: (name, op, operand1, operand2)
    
    operations = []
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        # Split on ":="
        parts = line.split(" := ", 1)
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        # If it's a constant, it's a single string
        # If it's an operation, it's "A op B"
        
        # Split by spaces
        expr_parts = expr.split()
        
        if len(expr_parts) == 1:
            # Constant
            operations.append((var_name, "const", expr_parts[0]))
        else:
            # Operation
            if len(expr_parts) == 3:
                first, op, second = expr_parts[0], expr_parts[1], expr_parts[2]
                operations.append((var_name, op, first, second))
    
    # For each bit position
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    for bit in range(m):
        # Calculate the total sum value when Peter's bit is 0
        # and when Peter's bit is 1
        
        # For each variable, compute its value at this bit position
        def get_value(var_name, peter_bit):
            # Returns the bit value (0 or 1) of the variable at this position
            if var_name == "?" or var_name == "peter":
                return peter_bit
            
            # Find this variable in operations
            for var, op_type, *args in operations:
                if var == var_name:
                    if op_type == "const":
                        # This should be the constant string
                        # Extract the bit at 'bit' position
                        const_str = args[0]
                        return int(const_str[bit])
                    else:  # op_type is an operation
                        first, op, second = args
                        a_val = get_value(first, peter_bit)
                        b_val = get_value(second, peter_bit)
                        
                        if op == "AND":
                            return a_val & b_val
                        elif op == "OR":
                            return a_val | b_val
                        elif op == "XOR":
                            return a_val ^ b_val
            return 0
        
        # When Peter's bit is 0
        total_0 = 0
        for var_name, op_type, *args in operations:
            val = get_value(var_name, 0)
            total_0 += val * (1 << bit)
        
        # When Peter's bit is 1
        total_1 = 0
        for var_name, op_type, *args in operations:
            val = get_value(var_name, 1)
            total_1 += val * (1 << bit)
        
        # Choose bit that minimizes the sum
        if total_0 <= total_1:
            min_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
        
        # Choose bit that maximizes the sum
        if total_0 >= total_1:
            max_result.append('0')
        else:
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Output results
    print(''.join(min_result))
    print(''.join(max_result))

solve()

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit number
   - Result of bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's number
4. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the sum of all variable values
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the sum of all variable values

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, each bit position can be analyzed independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine how the value at that position contributes to the total sum
3. The contribution of each bit position depends only on the bit value at that position in Peter's chosen number
4. We can solve this bit by bit independently

## Algorithm

1. For each bit position j (0 to m-1), analyze what happens when Peter's bit at position j is 0 vs 1
2. For each operation type (AND, OR, XOR), determine how the result at bit j depends on Peter's bit and other operands
3. Calculate the total contribution to the sum when Peter's bit j is 0 vs 1
4. For each bit position:
   - If setting bit j to 1 gives lower sum, choose 1
   - If setting bit j to 1 gives higher sum, choose 1
   - If both give same sum, choose 0 (to get minimum number)

## Step-by-step Solution

Let me trace through the first example:
- a := 101 (constant)
- b := 011 (constant) 
- c := ? XOR b

For each bit position:
- Bit 0: 
  - When Peter's bit is 0: a[0]=1, b[0]=1, c[0]=0⊕1=1 → sum contribution = 1+1+1 = 3
  - When Peter's bit is 1: a[0]=1, b[0]=1, c[0]=1⊕1=0 → sum contribution = 1+1+0 = 2
  - Wait, this doesn't match. Let me recalculate.

Actually, let me reconsider: the total sum is the sum of all variable values regardless of position.

For each bit position independently, we can calculate the contribution to the total sum when Peter's bit at that position is 0 or 1.

For each bit position j:
- Calculate the sum of values across all variables when Peter's bit j is 0
- Calculate the sum of values across all variables when Peter's bit j is 1
- If 0 gives lower sum, choose 0; if 1 gives lower sum, choose 1; if equal, choose 0

The key insight is that each bit position contributes independently to the total sum.

```python
        var_bits = {}

                # Get bits for first and second
                first_bit_0 = 0
                first_bit_1 = 0
                second_bit_0 = 0
                second_bit_1 = 0
                
                if first == '?':
                    first_bit_0 = 0
                    first_bit_1 = 1
                else:
                    # first is a variable name
                    if first in bit_val_0:
                        first_bit_0 = bit_val_0[first]
                    else:
                        # This should not happen as variables are defined in order
                        first_bit_0 = 0
                    if first in bit_val_1:
                        first_bit_1 = bit_val_1[first]
                    else:
                        first_bit_1 = 0
                
                if second == '?':
                    second_bit_0 = 0
                    second_bit_1 = 1
                else:
                    if second in bit_val_0:
                        second_bit_0 = bit_val_0[second]
                    else:
                        second_bit_0 = 0
                    if second in bit_val_1:
                        second_bit_1 = bit_val_1[second]
                    else:
                        second_bit_1 = 0
                
                # Perform operation
                if op == "AND":
                    bit_val_0[var_name] = first_bit_0 & second_bit_0
                    bit_val_1[var_name] = first_bit_1 & second_bit_1
                elif op == "OR":
                    bit_val_0[var_name] = first_bit_0 | second_bit_0
                    bit_val_1[var_name] = first_bit_1 | second_bit_1
                elif op == "XOR":
                    bit_val_0[var_name] = first_bit_0 ^ second_bit_0
                    bit_val_1[var_name] = first_bit_1 ^ second_bit_1
        
        # Calculate total sum for each case
        total_sum_0 = sum(bit_val_0[var_name] for var_name in variables)
        total_sum_1 = sum(bit_val_1[var_name] for var_name in variables)
        
        # For minimum score: choose 0 if sum_0 <= sum_1, else 1
        # For maximum score: choose 1 if sum_1 >= sum_0, else 0
        # But if equal, choose minimum number, which means 0
        
        if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
            result_min.append("0")
            result_max.append("1" if total_sum_1 > total_sum_0 else "0")
        else:
            result_min.append("1")
            result_max.append("1")
    
    # Convert to binary strings with m digits
    min_result = ''.join(result_min)
    max_result = ''.join(result_max)
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)

# Actually, I need to fix the indexing - the above code has issues with variable lookup
# Let me rewrite with proper order processing

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store variable definitions
    definitions = {}
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        var_name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        if expr[0] == '0' or expr[0] == '1':
            # Constant
            definitions[var_name] = (expr, None, None)
        else:
            # Operation
            expr_parts = expr.split()
            first_op = expr_parts[0]
            op = expr_parts[1]
            second_op = expr_parts[2]
            definitions[var_name] = (first_op, op, second_op)
    
    # For each bit position
    min_choice = []
    max_choice = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Calculate sum when Peter's bit is 0
        sum_zero = 0
        # Calculate sum when Peter's bit is 1
        sum_one = 0
        
        # We need to evaluate variables in order of definition
        # For each variable, compute its bit at position bit_pos based on Peter's bit
        var_values_0 = {}
        var_values_1 = {}
        
        for var_name in definitions:
            first, op, second = definitions[var_name]
            
            # Get values for first and second operands
            first_val_0 = 0
            first_val_1 = 0
            second_val_0 = 0
            second_val_1 = 0
            
            if first == '?':
                first_val_0 = 0
                first_val_1 = 1
            else:
                # first is a variable name
                if first in var_values_0:
                    first_val_0 = var_values_0[first]
                else:
                    # Should have been defined before
                    pass
                if first in var_values_1:
                    first_val_1 = var_values_1[first]
                else:
                    pass
            
            if second == '?':
                second_val_0 = 0
                second_val_1 = 1
            else:
                if second in var_values_0:
                    second_val_0 = var_values_0[second]
                else:
                    pass
                if second in var_values_1:
                    second_val_1 = var_values_1[second]
                else:
                    pass
            
            # Perform operation
            if op == "AND":
                var_values_0[var_name] = first_val_0 & second_val_0
                var_values_1[var_name] = first_val_1 & second_val_1
            elif op == "OR":
                var_values_0[var_name] = first_val_0 | second_val_0
                var_values_1[var_name] = first_val_1 | second_val_1
            elif op == "XOR":
                var_values_0[var_name] = first_val_0 ^ second_val_0
                var_values_1[var_name] = first_val_1 ^ second_val_1
        
        # Sum up contributions
        sum_zero = sum(var_values_0[var_name] for var_name in definitions)
        sum_one = sum(var_values_1[var_name] for var_name in definitions)
        
        # For minimum score: choose bit value that minimizes sum
        # If 0 gives <= 1, choose 0, else choose 1 (and if equal, choose minimum which is 0)
        if sum_zero <= sum_one:
            min_choice.append("0")
            max_choice.append("1" if sum_one > sum_zero else "0")
        else:
            min_choice.append("1")
            max_choice.append("1")
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary
    min_result = ''.join(min_choice)
    max_result = ''.join(max_choice)
    
    print(min_result)
    print(max_result)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.


Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an `m`-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between existing variables or Peter's number
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total sum
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total sum

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently due to the properties of bitwise operations
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 gives a smaller or larger total sum
3. The total sum is the sum of all variable values, and we can compute the contribution of each bit position separately

## Approach

1. Parse the input to get the variable definitions
2. For each bit position from 0 to m-1:
   - Simulate the computation with bit set to 0
   - Simulate the computation with bit set to 1
   - Determine which value gives smaller/larger total sum
3. Build the final answer by combining the bit decisions

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store variable definitions
    variables = {}  # variable_name -> (expression, dependencies)
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(":=")
        name = parts[0].strip()
        expr = parts[1].strip()
        
        # Parse the expression
        expr_parts = expr.split()
        # The first part is the first operand
        first_operand = expr_parts[0]
        # The second part is the operation
        op = expr_parts[1]
        # The third part is the second operand
        second_operand = expr_parts[2]
        
        variables[name] = (first_operand, op, second_operand)
    
    # For each bit position, determine optimal choice
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # For this bit position, we'll evaluate the total sum when Peter's bit is 0 vs 1
        # We need to simulate the computation for all variables
        
        # Current value of Peter's chosen bit for this position
        p_bit_0 = 0
        p_bit_1 = 1
        
        # Function to evaluate a variable given Peter's bit at current position
        def evaluate_var(var_name, p_bit):
            if var_name in variables:
                first_op, op, second_op = variables[var_name]
                
                # Get the value of first operand
                if first_op == '?':
                    first_val = p_bit
                else:
                    first_val = int(first_op[bit_pos])
                
                # Get the value of second operand
                if second_op == '?':
                    second_val = p_bit
                else:
                    second_val = int(second_op[bit_pos])
                
                # Apply operation
                if op == 'AND':
                    result = first_val & second_val
                elif op == 'OR':
                    result = first_val | second_val
                elif op == 'XOR':
                    result = first_val ^ second_val
                
                return result
            else:
                # Base case - this shouldn't happen
                return 0
        
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 0
        total_sum_0 = 0
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            total_sum_0 += evaluate_var(var_name, p_bit_0)
        
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 1  
        total_sum_1 = 0
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            total_sum_1 += evaluate_var(var_name, p_bit_1)
        
        # For minimizing sum: choose the bit that gives smaller sum
        if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
            min_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
        
        # For maximizing sum: choose the bit that gives larger sum
        if total_sum_0 >= total_sum_1:
            max_result.append('0')
        else:
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary strings
    min_result_str = ''.join(min_result)
    max_result_str = ''.join(max_result)
    
    print(min_result_str)
    print(max_result_str)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Store variable definitions
    variables = {}
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(":=")
        name = parts[0].strip()
        expr = parts[1].strip()
        
        # Parse the expression
        expr_parts = expr.split()
        first_operand = expr_parts[0]
        op = expr_parts[1]
        second_operand = expr_parts[2]
        
        variables[name] = (first_operand, op, second_operand)
    
    # For each bit position, determine optimal choice
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    # Process each bit position from most significant to least significant
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Function to evaluate a variable given Peter's bit at current position
        def evaluate_var(var_name, p_bit):
            if var_name in variables:
                first_op, op, second_op = variables[var_name]
                
                # Get the value of first operand (0 or 1)
                if first_op == '?':
                    first_val = p_bit
                else:
                    # Extract the bit at position bit_pos (0-indexed from left)
                    first_val = int(first_op[bit_pos])
                
                # Get the value of second operand
                if second_op == '?':
                    second_val = p_bit
                else:
                    second_val = int(second_op[bit_pos])
                
                # Apply operation
                if op == 'AND':
                    result = first_val & second_val
                elif op == 'OR':
                    result = first_val | second_val
                elif op == 'XOR':
                    result = first_val ^ second_val
                
                return result
            else:
                return 0
        
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 0
        total_sum_0 = 0
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            total_sum_0 += evaluate_var(var_name, 0)
        
        # Calculate total sum when Peter's bit is 1
        total_sum_1 = 0
        for var_name in variables.keys():
            total_sum_1 += evaluate_var(var_name, 1)
        
        # For minimizing sum: choose the bit that gives smaller sum
        if total_sum_0 <= total_sum_1:
            min_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
        
        # For maximizing sum: choose the bit that gives larger sum
        if total_sum_0 >= total_sum_1:
            max_result.append('0')
        else:
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary strings
    min_result_str = ''.join(min_result)
    max_result_str = ''.join(max_result)
    
    print(min_result_str)
    print(max_result_str)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or the '?' symbol
4. Peter's score is the sum of all variable values
5. We need to find:
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to minimize the total sum
   - The minimum number Peter should choose to maximize the total sum

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations and we want to minimize/maximize the sum, we can analyze each bit position independently.

2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 gives a lower or higher contribution to the total sum.

3. The total sum is the sum of all variable values, and each variable value contributes to the sum based on its bit positions.

4. For each bit position, we can calculate the contribution of that bit position to the total sum when Peter's bit is 0 vs when it's 1.

5. Since bitwise operations are linear in each bit position, we can analyze each bit position separately.

## Solution Strategy

1. For each bit position (from 0 to m-1), we'll calculate the contribution of that bit to the total sum when Peter's bit is 0 vs when it's 1.

2. For each variable, when we evaluate it, we determine which bits are set based on the input operands and the operation.

3. For each bit position, we compute:
   - Total contribution when Peter's bit is 0
   - Total contribution when Peter's bit is 1

4. If setting Peter's bit to 1 gives a lower total contribution, we prefer 1; otherwise, we prefer 0.

5. We do this for each bit position independently and combine the results.

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
    operations = []

                bit1 = 0
                bit2 = 0

                        bit1 = 1
                    else:
                        bit1 = 0

                # If op2 is a constant, use its bit value
                if op2 != '?':
                    if op2[bit_pos] == '1':
                        bit2 = 1
                    else:
                        bit2 = 0
                else:
                    bit2 = 0  # Peter's bit is 0

                    result_bit = bit1 & bit2
                elif op == "OR":
                    result_bit = bit1 | bit2
                elif op == "XOR":

                        bit2 = 1

        # Since each bit contributes 2^bit_pos to the total sum, we multiply by 2^bit_pos
        bit_contribution_0 = sum_0 * (2 ** bit_pos)
        bit_contribution_1 = sum_1 * (2 ** bit_pos)
        
        # For minimum total sum, choose the value of Peter's bit that minimizes the sum
        if bit_contribution_0 <= bit_contribution_1:
            min_result += '0'
        else:
            min_result += '1'
            
        # For maximum total sum, choose the value of Peter's bit that maximizes the sum
        if bit_contribution_1 >= bit_contribution_0:
            max_result += '1'
        else:
            max_result += '0'
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----

3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

bb := 0

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is either:
   - A constant m-bit number, or
   - Result of bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or Peter's number
4. We need to find:
   - The minimum Peter can choose to minimize the sum of all variable values
   - The minimum Peter can choose to maximize the sum of all variable values
5. If multiple choices give same score, choose the smallest binary number

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine whether setting that bit to 0 or 1 minimizes/maximizes the total sum
3. The operations are linear in a way that allows independent bit analysis
4. We need to evaluate the contribution of each bit position to the final sum

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Parse the input**: Read variable definitions
2. **For each bit position**: Simulate what happens if we set that bit to 0 or 1
3. **Calculate contribution**: For each possible bit value (0 or 1) at each position, calculate the total sum
4. **Track minimum/maximum**: For each position, determine whether 0 or 1 gives better scores
5. **Construct result**: Build the final binary numbers

## Implementation Strategy

Since bitwise operations are bit-by-bit independent, we can process each bit position separately:

- For each bit position `i` (0 to m-1), we consider two scenarios: bit value = 0 and bit value = 1
- For each scenario, we compute the total sum contribution from all variables
- We choose 0 if it gives smaller sum, or if equal, we prefer 0 (since we want minimum number)
- Similarly for maximum sum

```python
                    op_pos = expr.find(op)

                    op2 = expr[op_pos + len(op):].strip()

                    break

        # if Peter sets bit at position bit_pos to 0 or 1

        def get_var_value(var_name, peter_bit, bit_pos):

                result = val1 & val2

    all_rules = []
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()

        var_name = parts[0].strip()
        expr = parts[1].strip()
        
        # Parse the expression
        # Look for operators
        op_type = None
        left_operand = None
        right_operand = None
        
        # Check if it's a constant
        if len(expr) == m:  # It's a constant of m bits
            constants = {
                "AND": "AND",
                "OR": "OR",
                "XOR": "XOR"
            }
            for op in constants:
                if op in expr:
                    # Find the position of the op
                    op_pos = expr.find(op)
                    left_operand = expr[:op_pos].strip()
                    right_operand = expr[op_pos + len(op):].strip()
                    op_type = op
                    break
            else:
                # No operation, it's a constant
                variables[var_name] = (None, None, None)
        
        # Otherwise, it's an operation
        if op_type is None:
            # Try to find the operation
            for op in ["AND", "OR", "XOR"]:
                if op in expr:
                    op_pos = expr.find(op)
                    left_operand = expr[:op_pos].strip()
                    right_operand = expr[op_pos + len(op):].strip()
                    op_type = op
                    break
        
        if op_type is not None:
            variables[var_name] = (left_operand, right_operand, op_type)
        else:
            variables[var_name] = (None, None, None)
    
    # Now for each bit position, calculate the sum contribution
    min_choice = []
    max_choice = []
    
    # Process each bit position independently
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # Try both choices for Peter's bit at this position
        score_0 = 0
        score_1 = 0
        
        # Function to evaluate all variables given Peter's bit at this position
        def evaluate_all(peter_bit):
            # We need to compute the value of each variable at this bit position
            values = {}
            
            # Process variables in order
            for var_name in variables:
                op1, op2, op_type = variables[var_name]
                
                val1 = 0
                val2 = 0
                
                if op1 == '?':
                    val1 = peter_bit
                else:
                    val1 = values.get(op1, 0)
                
                if op2 == '?':
                    val2 = peter_bit
                else:
                    val2 = values.get(op2, 0)
                
                if op_type == "AND":
                    val = val1 & val2
                elif op_type == "OR":
                    val = val1 | val2
                elif op_type == "XOR":
                    val = val1 ^ val2
                
                values[var_name] = val
            
            # Sum all values
            total_sum = sum(values.values())
            return total_sum
        
        # Calculate scores for both choices
        score_0 = evaluate_all(0)
        score_1 = evaluate_all(1)
        
        # Determine choice for minimum sum
        if score_0 < score_1:
            min_choice.append('0')
        elif score_0 > score_1:
            min_choice.append('1')
        else:
            # Same score, choose minimum number (0)
            min_choice.append('0')
        
        # Determine choice for maximum sum
        if score_0 > score_1:
            max_choice.append('0')
        elif score_0 < score_1:
            max_choice.append('1')
        else:
            # Same score, choose minimum number (0)
            max_choice.append('0')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary strings (add leading zeros)
    print(''.join(min_choice))
    print(''.join(max_choice))
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` variables and `m` bit depth
2. Peter chooses an m-bit number (represented by '?')
3. Each variable is defined as either:
   - A constant m-bit binary number
   - A bitwise operation (AND, OR, XOR) between previous variables or the '?' symbol
4. We need to find:
   - The minimum Peter can choose to minimize the sum of all variable values
   - The minimum Peter can choose to maximize the sum of all variable values

## Key Insights

1. Since we're dealing with bitwise operations, we can analyze each bit position independently
2. For each bit position (0 to m-1), we can determine how the value at that position affects the total sum
3. For each bit position, we can compute the contribution to the sum based on whether Peter chooses 0 or 1 at that position
4. Since the operations are bitwise, the result at each position is independent of other positions

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse Input and Store Variable Definitions
- Read n and m
- For each variable, parse its definition
- Store the operations and operands

### Step 2: For Each Bit Position, Analyze the Effect
- For each bit position from 0 to m-1:
  - Simulate what happens if Peter chooses 0 at that position vs 1 at that position
  - Calculate the sum contribution from all variables at that bit position
  - Determine which choice (0 or 1) gives better total sum

### Step 3: Build Final Result
- For minimum sum: choose 0 when it gives lower sum, otherwise 1 (and if equal, prefer 0)
- For maximum sum: choose 1 when it gives higher sum, otherwise 0 (and if equal, prefer 0)

### Step 4: Implementation Strategy
- For each bit position, evaluate each variable's value when Peter's bit is 0 and when it's 1
- Accumulate the bit contribution to total sum
- Compare and make decisions

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    variables = {}
    definitions = []
    
    for _ in range(n):
        line = input().strip()
        parts = line.split(" := ")
        name = parts[0]
        expr = parts[1]
        
        # Parse the expression
        expr = expr.strip()
        # Find the operation
        if " " not in expr:
            # This is a constant
            const = expr
            definitions.append((name, const, None, None))
        else:
            # Find the operation
            op_index = expr.find(" ")
            if " " not in expr[op_index:]:
                # No operation found, this can't happen based on problem
                pass
            else:
                # Split the expression
                op_parts = expr.split(" ")
                # Need to find where the operation is
                op_idx = -1
                for i in range(len(op_parts)):
                    if op_parts[i] in ["AND", "OR", "XOR"]:
                        op_idx = i
                        break
                
                if op_idx == -1:
                    # Shouldn't happen
                    const = expr
                    definitions.append((name, const, None, None))
                else:
                    first_operand = op_parts[op_idx - 1] if op_idx > 0 else op_parts[0]
                    op = op_parts[op_idx]
                    second_operand = op_parts[op_idx + 1] if op_idx + 1 < len(op_parts) else ""
                    
                    definitions.append((name, first_operand, op, second_operand))
    
    # For each bit position, determine the optimal choice for Peter
    min_result = []
    max_result = []
    
    for bit_pos in range(m):
        # We need to evaluate each variable for each possible value of Peter's bit at this position
        # For each variable, we need to know if it depends on Peter's bit
        
        # Store the contribution to sum when Peter's bit is 0 vs 1
        sum_when_zero = 0
        sum_when_one = 0
        
        # Evaluate all variables with Peter's bit = 0 at this position
        # First, build the variable values with Peter's bit = 0
        
        # We need to know the value of each variable at each bit position
        # For each variable, we'll compute its value when Peter has bit 'p' at current position
        
        # Process all variables in order
        # For each variable, compute its bit value at the current position
        # when Peter chooses 0 and when Peter chooses 1
        
        # Create a function to evaluate a variable's value at bit position
        def evaluate_bit(p_bit):
            # Returns the bit value at bit_pos for this variable
            # This function is called for each variable definition
            
            # We need to evaluate all variable definitions in order
            # Store the bit value for each variable
            var_bits = {}
            
            for var_name, first_operand, op, second_operand in definitions:
                if var_name not in var_bits:
                    var_bits[var_name] = 0
                
                # Special case: constant
                if op is None:
                    # This is a constant
                    const_bits = first_operand
                    bit_val = int(const_bits[bit_pos])
                    var_bits[var_name] = bit_val
                else:
                    # It's an operation
                    # Find the bit values of operands
                    first_val = 0
                    second_val = 0
                    
                    # First operand
                    if first_operand == '?':
                        first_val = p_bit
                    elif first_operand in var_bits:
                        first_val = var_bits[first_operand]
                    else:
                        # This shouldn't happen
                        first_val = 0
                    
                    # Second operand
                    if second_operand == '?':
                        second_val = p_bit
                    elif second_operand in var_bits:
                        second_val = var_bits[second_operand]
                    else:
                        # This shouldn't happen
                        second_val = 0
                    
                    # Apply operation
                    if op == "AND":
                        result_bit = first_val & second_val
                    elif op == "OR":
                        result_bit = first_val | second_val
                    elif op == "XOR":
                        result_bit = first_val ^ second_val
                    
                    var_bits[var_name] = result_bit
            
            # Return the value of each variable at this bit position
            return var_bits
        
        # Evaluate with Peter's bit = 0
        var_bits_0 = evaluate_bit(0)
        
        # Evaluate with Peter's bit = 1
        var_bits_1 = evaluate_bit(1)
        
        # Calculate sum of all variable values at this bit position
        sum_0 = 0
        sum_1 = 0
        
        for var_name, first_operand, op, second_operand in definitions:
            if op is None:
                # Constant
                const_bits = first_operand
                bit_val = int(const_bits[bit_pos])
                sum_0 += bit_val
                sum_1 += bit_val
            else:
                # Operation
                first_val = 0
                second_val = 0
                
                if first_operand == '?':
                    first_val = 0
                elif first_operand in var_bits_0:
                    first_val = var_bits_0[first_operand]
                else:
                    first_val = 0
                    
                if second_operand == '?':
                    second_val = 0
                elif second_operand in var_bits_0:
                    second_val = var_bits_0[second_operand]
                else:
                    second_val = 0
                    
                # Calculate the bit value for 0
                if op == "AND":
                    bit_val_0 = first_val & second_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    bit_val_0 = first_val | second_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    bit_val_0 = first_val ^ second_val
                    
                sum_0 += bit_val_0
                
                # Calculate the bit value for 1
                first_val_1 = 0
                second_val_1 = 0
                
                if first_operand == '?':
                    first_val_1 = 1
                elif first_operand in var_bits_1:
                    first_val_1 = var_bits_1[first_operand]
                else:
                    first_val_1 = 0
                    
                if second_operand == '?':
                    second_val_1 = 1
                elif second_operand in var_bits_1:
                    second_val_1 = var_bits_1[second_operand]
                else:
                    second_val_1 = 0
                    
                if op == "AND":
                    bit_val_1 = first_val_1 & second_val_1
                elif op == "OR":
                    bit_val_1 = first_val_1 | second_val_1
                elif op == "XOR":
                    bit_val_1 = first_val_1 ^ second_val_1
                    
                sum_1 += bit_val_1
        
        # Now we know the sum at this bit position for Peter choosing 0 or 1
        # For minimum sum, we want the smaller sum
        # For maximum sum, we want the larger sum
        if sum_0 <= sum_1:
            min_result.append('0')
            max_result.append('0')
        else:
            min_result.append('1')
            max_result.append('1')
    
    # Convert to m-bit binary strings
    min_result_str = ''.join(min_result)
    max_result_str = ''.join(max_result)
    
    print(min_result_str)
    print(max_result_str)

solve()
```

```python
n, m = map(int, input().split())

variables = []
definitions = []

for _ in range(n):
    line = input().strip()
    parts = line.split(" := ")
    name = parts[0]
    expr = parts[1]
    
    # Parse the expression
    op_part = None
    first_operand = None
    second_operand = None
    
    if " " not in expr:
        # Constant
        first_operand = expr
        op_part = None
    else:
        # Find the operation
        op_index = -1
        for i, c in enumerate(expr):
            if c == " ":
                op_index = i
                break
        
        if op_index == -1:
            first_operand = expr
            op_part = None
        else:
            # Split by spaces
            expr_parts = expr.split(" ")
            # Find operation
            op_found = False
            for i, part in enumerate(expr_parts):
                if part in ["AND", "OR", "XOR"]:
                    op_part = part
                    first_operand = expr_parts[i-1] if i > 0 else expr_parts[0]
                    second_operand = expr_parts[i+1] if i+1 < len(expr_parts) else ""
                    op_found = True
                    break
            
            if not op_found:
                first_operand = expr
                op_part = None
    
    definitions.append((name, first_operand, op_part, second_operand))

min_result = []

# Process each bit position
for bit_pos in range(m):
    # Calculate sum when Peter chooses 0 at this bit position
    sum_when_zero = 0
    # Calculate sum when Peter chooses 1 at this bit position  
    sum_when_one = 0
    
    # First, evaluate what each variable would be with Peter's bit = 0
    # For each variable, compute its bit value at current position
    
    def eval_with_choice(choice):
        # Returns the bit value of each variable at current bit position
        var_bits = {}
        
        for var_name, first_operand, op, second_operand in definitions:
            if var_name not in var_bits:
                var_bits[var_name] = 0
            
            if op is None:
                # Constant
                const_bits = first_operand
                var_bits[var_name] = int(const_bits[bit_pos])
            else:
                # Operation
                first_val = 0
                second_val = 0
                
                if first_operand == '?':
                    first_val = choice
                elif first_operand in var_bits:
                    first_val = var_bits[first_operand]
                else:
                    first_val = 0

                    second_val = choice
                elif second_operand in var_bits:
                    second_val = var_bits[second_operand]
                else:
                    second_val = 0
                    
                if op == "AND":
                    var_bits[var_name] = first_val & second_val
                elif op == "OR":
                    var_bits[var_name] = first_val | second_val
                elif op == "XOR":
                    var_bits[var_name] = first_val ^ second_val
        
        return var_bits
    
    # Get the bit values when Peter chooses 0
    var_bits_0 = eval_with_choice(0)
    
    # Get the bit values when Peter chooses 1
    var_bits_1 = eval_with_choice(1)
    
    # Calculate sum for each case
    for var_name, first_operand, op, second_operand in definitions:
        if op is None:
            # Constant
            const_bits = first_operand
            bit_val_0 = int(const_bits[bit_pos])
            bit_val_1 = int(const_bits[bit_pos])
            sum_when_zero += bit_val_0
            sum_when_one += bit_val_1
        else:
            # Operation
            # For case 0
            first_val_0 = 0
            second_val_0 = 0
            
            if first_operand == '?':
                first_val_0 = 0
            elif first_operand in var_bits_0:
                first_val_0 = var_bits_0[first_operand]
            else:
                first_val_0 = 0
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Bob recently read about bitwise operations used in computers: AND, OR and XOR. He have studied their properties and invented a new game.

Initially, Bob chooses integer m, bit depth of the game, which means that all numbers in the game will consist of m bits. Then he asks Peter to choose some m-bit number. After that, Bob computes the values of n variables. Each variable is assigned either a constant m-bit number or result of bitwise operation. Operands of the operation may be either variables defined before, or the number, chosen by Peter. After that, Peter's score equals to the sum of all variable values.

Bob wants to know, what number Peter needs to choose to get the minimum possible score, and what number he needs to choose to get the maximum possible score. In both cases, if there are several ways to get the same score, find the minimum number, which he can choose.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m, the number of variables and bit depth, respectively (1 ≤ n ≤ 5000; 1 ≤ m ≤ 1000). 

The following n lines contain descriptions of the variables. Each line describes exactly one variable. Description has the following format: name of a new variable, space, sign ":=", space, followed by one of:  Binary number of exactly m bits.  The first operand, space, bitwise operation ("AND", "OR" or "XOR"), space, the second operand. Each operand is either the name of variable defined before or symbol '?', indicating the number chosen by Peter.  

Variable names are strings consisting of lowercase Latin letters with length at most 10. All variable names are different.


-----Output-----

In the first line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values minimum possible, in the second line output the minimum number that should be chosen by Peter, to make the sum of all variable values maximum possible. Both numbers should be printed as m-bit binary numbers.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 3
a := 101
b := 011
c := ? XOR b

Output
011
100

Input
5 1
a := 1
bb := 0
cx := ? OR a
d := ? XOR ?
e := d AND bb

Output
0
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample if Peter chooses a number 011_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 000_2, the sum of their values is 8. If he chooses the number 100_2, then a = 101_2, b = 011_2, c = 111_2, the sum of their values is 15.

For the second test, the minimum and maximum sum of variables a, bb, cx, d and e is 2, and this sum doesn't depend on the number chosen by Peter, so the minimum Peter can choose is 0.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
