I'll solve this step-by-step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to sort a string where each word contains a single number (1-9)
2. The number in each word indicates its position in the sorted result
3. Position 1 comes first, position 2 comes second, etc.
4. If input is empty, return empty string
5. Each word contains exactly one consecutive number from 1-9

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify the approach
- Split the input string into words
- For each word, extract the number (digits 1-9) to determine its position
- Sort the words based on their numbers
- Join them back into a string

### Step 2: Extract numbers from words
- We need to find the digit in each word
- Since each word has exactly one digit, we can iterate through each character
- When we find a digit (1-9), that's the position

### Step 3: Sort according to positions
- Create a list of (position, word) pairs
- Sort by position
- Extract just the words in order

### Step 4: Handle edge cases
- Empty string returns empty string

## Implementation

```python
def order(sentence):
    if not sentence:
        return ""
    
    # Split the sentence into words
    words = sentence.split()
    
    # Create list of (position, word) pairs
    word_positions = []
    
    for word in words:
        # Find the digit in the word (1-9)
        for char in word:
            if char.isdigit():
                position = int(char)
                word_positions.append((position, word))
                break
    
    # Sort by position
    word_positions.sort()
    
    # Extract just the words in sorted order
    result = " ".join(word for position, word in word_positions)
    
    return result
```

## Trace through examples:

Example 1: "is2 Thi1s T4est 3a"
- "is2" → has '2' → position 2
- "Thi1s" → has '1' → position 1  
- "T4est" → has '4' → position 4
- "3a" → has '3' → position 3
- Sorted by position: position 1, 2, 3, 4 → "Thi1s is2 3a T4est"

Example 2: "4of Fo1r pe6ople g3ood th5e the2"
- "4of" → has '4' → position 4
- "Fo1r" → has '1' → position 1
- "pe6ople" → has '6' → position 6
- "g3ood" → has '3' → position 3
- "th5e" → has '5' → position 5
- "the2" → has '2' → position 2
- Sorted: position 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 → "Fo1r the2 g3ood 4of th5e pe6ople"

The solution works correctly!

I'll solve this step-by-step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to sort a string where each word contains a single number (1-9)
2. The number in each word indicates the position (1-indexed) that word should have in the result
3. Words without numbers should be placed in their correct position based on their number
4. If input is empty, return empty string

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify the pattern
- Each word contains exactly one digit (1-9) that represents its position
- We need to extract the number from each word and sort the words by that number
- Position 1 comes first, then position 2, etc.

### Step 2: Algorithm design
1. Split the input string into words
2. For each word, find the digit (1-9) in it
3. Create a list of (number, word) pairs
4. Sort this list by the number
5. Join the sorted words back into a string

### Step 3: Implementation details
- Extract the digit from each word: iterate through characters until we find a digit
- Since each word has exactly one consecutive number, we can find it by checking each character
- Sort by the extracted number (which will be 1-9)

### Step 4: Edge cases
- Empty string: return empty string
- Single word: just return that word in its correct position
- Words with no numbers: the problem states each word contains a single number, so this shouldn't happen

## Code Implementation

```python
def order(sentence):
    if not sentence:
        return ""
    
    # Split the sentence into words
    words = sentence.split()
    
    # Create list of (number, word) pairs
    word_with_number = []
    
    for word in words:
        # Find the digit in the word (1-9)
        for char in word:
            if char.isdigit():
                number = int(char)
                word_with_number.append((number, word))
                break
    
    # Sort by the number (position)
    word_with_number.sort()
    
    # Extract just the words in sorted order
    result = " ".join(word for number, word in word_with_number)
    
    return result
```
